However LSE with β large may be ill-conditioned, gradient dominated by max convolution positions. Could use iterative active-set projection:
- Start normalized a.
- For iteration:
  1. Compute c = conv(a,a).
  2. Let M = max(c). Choose weights p_k = exp(β(c_k - M)) (softmax), β adaptive.
  3. Compute h_i = (p * a)_i + (p^R * a)_i. This is directional derivative of smooth max at step: g' = h·v /2? Actually if a->a+εv, c' weighted = ∑ p_k (v*a)_k +(a*v)_k? = 2∑ h_i v_i? Need factor.
  4. Choose v to reduce h while staying in simplex. Maybe v = -h + mean? Then project.
  Simpler: set a ← a - lr*h, clip, normalize. But h positive; will reduce all entries? h_i roughly weighted interaction. We need keep sum normalization.
  Since scale invariant, can do without constraints? If we subtract positive h, some entries die. Need careful.

Alternative use multiplicative updates. For nonnegative a, objective max conv. There are multiplicative power methods for minimizing max? Since c = a^T P_k a. Maybe update a_i ∝ a_i * exp(-lr h_i). Let's consider direct discrete optimization from sqrt init using active-set linearization. We can implement simpler "water-filling" iter:
- Normalize a sum=1.

- Best a, score.

     * active excess: w = relu(c - avg +?) / sum
  For each weight, compute grad g = correlate(w,a)[offset]; force = a*g; normalize force direction; produce candidate a2 = a * exp(-eta*(force-mean_force)) / sum. Maybe also additive candidate a2 = a + eta2*(mean_g - g) clip.
  - Evaluate candidate exact score; accept best if < best_score.
  - If no improvement for many, perturb (random block multiplicative) or change weights.

Let's design code structure:
- Constants: budget 1800, reserve 5s.
- Helper `fast_score(a)`: uses np.convolve maybe for current search; for normalized sum=1, but for general use sum.
```
def fast_score(a):
    n=len(a)
    s=a.sum()
    if s <= 0: return inf
    c=np.convolve(a,a)
    nxn=c.max()
    return 2.0*n*nxn/(s*s)
```
This is exact same as evaluate but without list conversion/security; for internal. For final best, maybe call evaluate_sequence to validate? We can call, but not necessary. It may be expensive once. We can call on final candidates to ensure no invalid and get official score. But if our fast score differs due to float rounding? okay.

- Helper `normalize(a)`: a=a/sum; return.

- Helper `corr_weight(w,a)`:
```
def weight_gradient(w,a):
    n=len(a)
    # w length 2n-1, nonnegative, sum maybe 1
    cor = np.correlate(w, a, mode='full')
    return cor[n-1:2*n-1]
```

- Helper `local_search(a, budget, seed, verbose)`:
  - Normalize a. best=a, best_score=fast_score.
  - rng.
  - Track iterations, time.
  - Parameters: `eta = 0.5`, `eta_decay`, `p_mode` cycle [1,2,3,5,8,16,inf], temperature.
  - `stall=0`.
  - Loop while time left:
    * n=len(a); c=np.convolve(a,a); M=c.max(); current_score=2*n*M (since sum 1) (use fast_score maybe). If current_score < best_score update best.
    * If time near deadline break.
    * Choose weight mode. Let's implement p-norm weights:
      - If mode == 'l1': w = c (sum c=1)
      - 'l2': w = c*c / sum(c*c)
      - 'excess': w = np.maximum(c - c.mean(), 0); if sum>0 normalize else w uniform
      - 'max': T = max((M - np.median(c))*0.5, M*1e-3); w = exp((c-M)/T); normalize. But if T too small, underflow; okay. To avoid all zero, subtract max.
    We can cycle modes based on iteration and stall. Initially use 'excess' or 'l2' to shape, then 'max'.
    * Compute g = weight_gradient(w, a). Since w normalized sum1? For p-norm, we can avoid normalizing? Force scales with w; we can normalize weights to sum1 to control step. Do `w = w / w.sum()`.
    * force = a * g. Compute meanf = np.mean(force). scale = np.std(force) or np.mean(np.abs(force-meanf)) + eps. dir = (force - meanf)/scale. Candidate log update: `a2 = a * np.exp(-eta * dir)`. Normalize. Ensure finite; if not use additive.
    * Also maybe second candidate with mode 'g equalization': `delta = np.mean(g)-g`; scale = np.std(g); a2 = a + eta_g*delta/scale; clip. But maybe log enough.
    * Line search eta: maintain global eta. Candidate score = fast_score(a2) (convolve). If < best_score * (1-1e-6) or < current_score? Accept if improves best. If improves current, set a=a2, stall=0, maybe increase eta *=1.05. Else rollback, eta*=0.95, stall +=1. If stall > 50, random perturbation: multiply random blocks or smooth noise, normalize, eta reset. Also if no improvement for long, try changing initial or mode.
    * To avoid high cost, each iteration two convolves: current c (for weights) and candidate score. Current c can be reused for weight; candidate score must convolve. So ~2 conv per iter. Good.
    * We can update best if candidate score better, even if current score same.
  - Return best, score.

Alternatively, generate parameterized family and do 1D scans at full n using fast_score. For each n, try p values 0.3-0.5, lam values, rational c. This is cheap (each score one convolve). We can find good initial before iterative.

Let's implement initial scan:
```
def scan_initials(n, time_limit):
    candidates=[]
    for p in [0.0,0.1,0.2,0.3,0.35,0.4,0.45,0.49,0.5]:
       candidates.append(power)
    for lam in [-0.5,-0.2,0,0.2,0.5]:
       candidates.append(power+lam*linear)
    for c in [0,1,3,10]: rational
    for alpha in [0,0.1,0.3,0.5,1.0]: two_power
    evaluate with fast_score, keep top.
```
Fast_score each candidate convolve O(n²). For n=4096, 30 candidates ~30*16M=480M ops maybe seconds (maybe 10s). Acceptable. For n=8192 maybe 30*67M=2B too slow. Limit scans for n<=4096.

```
def evaluate_x(c0, r, self_idx, S0, x, n, constant? included maybe no 2n):
    # compute M
    if self_idx >=0:
        vals = c0 + r*x
        vals[self_idx] += x*x  # because r[self_idx] doesn't include self? yes
    else: vals=c0+r*x
    return vals.max() / ((S0+x)*(S0+x))
```

But coordinate exact update must update c after choosing x. Instead of recomputing full conv O(n²), we can update c by adding contributions of delta = x_new - old. When changing i from old to x_new, c changes by:
For all k:

Because c_old = c0 + r old + old²δ; new = c0 + r new + new²δ. So update c in O(n) using r (now with b_i=0) and delta. Nice. Then a[i]=x_new. Sum updates by delta.
This makes coordinate pass very efficient: no full convolve each coordinate, only O(n) per coordinate. Great! Initial c via np.convolve once. Then coordinate descent passes O(n²) with low constant, much faster than gradient with convolve per iter. This is promising.

```
def val(x):
    if x == 0: M = c0.max()
    else:
       # compute max of c0 + r*x; avoid allocation? use np.max(c0 + r*x)
       # plus self
       m = np.max(c0 + r*x)
       if self_idx>=0:
          selfv = c0[self_idx] + r[self_idx]*x + x*x
          if selfv > m: m = selfv
       else: m = ...

Coordinate pass implementation details:
```
def coord_value(c0, h, self_base, S0, x, tmp):
    if x <= 0.0:
        m = np.max(c0)
    else:
        np.multiply(h, x, out=tmp)
        tmp += c0
        m = np.max(tmp)
    sv = self_base + x*x
    if sv > m: m = sv
    denom = S0 + x
    return m/(denom*denom)
```
But if `x` very small, x*x under? fine.
`tmp += c0` modifies tmp; in next call multiply overwrites all entries with h*x. However if h has zeros, multiply sets 0, then add c0. okay.

Golden min:
```
def minimize_coord(c0,h,self_idx,self_base,S0,old,n,tmp):
    # hi
    hi = max(S0*1.0, old*4.0, 10.0/n)
    if hi <= 0: hi=1.0
    # evaluate f0,fhi, f_old
    f_old = coord_value(...old)
    best_x=old; best_val=f_old
    # try 0
    f0=coord_value(...0)
    if f0<best_val: best_x=0; best_val=f0
    fhi=coord_value(...hi)
    if fhi<best_val: # extend? if lower, increase hi until higher
       x2=hi
       f2=fhi
       for _ in range(10):
           x2*=2
           f2=coord_value(...x2)
           if f2 > fhi or x2>1e6: break
           # keep lower as best
           if f2<best_val: best_x=x2; best_val=f2
       hi=x2
    # If fhi < f0 and fhi < f_old, the minimum may be beyond hi? But as x large f→1, if fhi still low, extend until fhi > best maybe. If hi huge and f large, bracket.
    # Now golden on [0,hi]
    a=0.0; b=hi
    gr = (sqrt5-1)/2
    c = b - gr*(b-a)
    d = a + gr*(b-a)
    fc=coord_value(c); fd=coord_value(d)
    for it in range(35):
       if b-a < 1e-8*max(1.0,b): break
       if fc <= fd:
          b=d; d=c; fd=fc; c=b - gr*(b-a); fc=coord_value(c)
       else:
          a=c; c=d; fc=fd; d=a + gr*(b-c); fd=coord_value(d)
    x=0.5*(a+b)
    fx=coord_value(x)
    if fx<best_val: best_x=x; best_val=fx
    # maybe local ternary? return best_x
```
But if fhi > f0, [0,hi] includes min. If f0 is global boundary, golden may explore whole interval but okay.

Let's write helpers:

```
import numpy as np
import time
import math

_GOLDEN = 0.6180339887498949

def _normalize_sequence(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    if a.ndim != 1 or a.size == 0:
        a = np.ones(64, dtype=np.float64)
    a[a < 0] = 0.0
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-15:
        return np.ones(64, dtype=np.float64)/64.0
    a /= s
    return a
```
But final values should not be clipped to 1000? normalized okay.

`_fast_score(a)`:
```
def _fast_score(a):
    a=np.asarray(a)
    n=a.size
    if n==0: return inf
    s=a.sum()
    if s<=0: return inf
    c=np.convolve(a,a)
    m=float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(m) or m<0: return inf
    return float(2.0*n*m/(s*s))
```
For coordinate_search, we don't want to call this often. Use initial.

`_make_power(n,p)`:
```
def _make_power(n,p):
    x=np.arange(n,dtype=np.float64)+1.0
    if p==0: a=np.ones(n)
    else: a=x**(-p)
    return _normalize_sequence(a)
```
`_make_rational(n,c)`: `1/ (np.sqrt(x)+c)`.
`_upsample(a,target)`:
```
def _upsample(a,target):
    a=np.asarray(a,float)
    m=a.size
    if m == target: return a.copy()
    if m == 1: return np.full(target, a[0])
    xo=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,m)
    xn=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,target)
    return _normalize_sequence(np.interp(xn,xo,a))
```
Could use fractional indices to preserve endpoint singular: `np.linspace(0,m-1)` not matter. Use 0..1.

Coordinate value: Need avoid closure. It uses `tmp`; if x==0: max c0. If x>0: `np.multiply(h, x, out=tmp); tmp += c0; m=float(tmp.max())`. But if h/c0 are contiguous, tmp same. Then `sv=self_base + x*x`. `denom=S0+x`; if denom <=0: return inf. return m/(denom*denom). Note value is g=M/S²; score=2n*g. We minimize g.

`_coordinate_search(a, max_time, seed, verbose)`:
```
def _coordinate_search(a, max_time, seed=1, verbose=False):
    start=time.monotonic()
    deadline=start+max(1.0, max_time)
    a=_normalize_sequence(a)
    n=a.size; N=2*n-1
    c=np.convolve(a,a).astype(np.float64)
    S=1.0
    best=a.copy(); best_score=2.0*n*float(np.max(c))
    if verbose: print(...)
    if n < 2: return best,best_score
    h=np.empty(N); c0=np.empty(N); buf=np.empty(N)
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    no_improve=0; passes=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
       passes +=1
       S = float(np.sum(a))
       if S <= 0: break
       scale=1.0/S; a*=scale; c*=scale*scale; S=1.0  # normalize at start pass
       best_score=2.0*n*float(np.max(c)) if lower
       order=rng.permutation(n)
       broke_time=False
       for idx,i in enumerate(order):
          if (idx & 63)==0 and time.monotonic() >= deadline:
             broke_time=True; break
          old=float(a[i])
          S0=S-old
          if S0 <= 1e-14:
             # if only this coordinate, cannot improve; continue
             continue
          self_idx=2*i
          h.fill(0.0)
          # Need 2*a; to avoid temporary, maybe np.multiply(a,2,out=buf)? But h slice addition.
          # Use buf to hold 2a? We can do h[i:i+n] += 2.0*a; This creates scalar*array temp? In numpy, 2.0*a creates temp. For speed, use buf:
          np.multiply(a, 2.0, out=buf)
          h[i:i+n] += buf
          h[self_idx] -= 2.0*old
          # c0 = c - h*old - old^2 self
          if old != 0.0:
             np.multiply(h, old, out=buf)
             c0 = c - buf? Need out c0: np.subtract(c, buf, out=c0)
             c0[self_idx] -= old*old
          else:
             c0[:] = c  # copy
          # Clamp negative
          if old !=0? always: np.maximum(c0,0.0,out=c0)
          self_base=float(c0[self_idx])
          x_new = _minimize_coordinate(c0,h,self_idx,self_base,S0,old,n,buf)  # use buf as tmp; note buf currently contains h*old from c0 calc, but value will overwrite.
          if not np.isfinite(x_new) or x_new < 0.0: x_new=old
          # If x_new very close, skip
          if abs(x_new-old) > 1e-14:
              delta=x_new-old
              S += delta
              if delta != 0.0:
                 np.multiply(h, delta, out=buf)
                 c += buf
                 c[self_idx] += x_new*x_new - old*old
              a[i]=x_new
          # optional clamp a[i] nonneg
       # after pass (or break)
       S = float(np.sum(a))
       if S <= 0: break
       scale=1.0/S; a*=scale; c*=scale*scale
       # c may have small negative; max unaffected
       score=2.0*n*float(np.max(c))
       if not np.isfinite(score): break
       if score < best_score*(1.0-1e-12):
           best=a.copy(); best_score=score; no_improve=0
       else:
           no_improve +=1
       if verbose: print(...)
       if no_improve >= 4: break
       if broke_time: break
    return best,best_score
```

def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1800, **kwargs):
    start=time.monotonic()
    budget=float(budget_s)-15.0
    if budget < 30: return _make_power(512,0.5).tolist()
    deadline=start+budget
    best=None; best_score=inf
    # optional evaluate height_sequence_1 quickly
    gs=globals().get('height_sequence_1')
    gs_len=0
    if gs is not None:
       try:
          arr=_normalize_sequence(np.asarray(gs, dtype=np.float64))
          gs_len=arr.size
          if 200 <= gs_len <= 10000:
             s=_fast_score(arr)
             if s<best_score:
                best=arr; best_score=s
       except Exception: pass
    stages=[]
    # define stages with fractions
    stages = [(1024,0.07),(2048,0.20),(4096,0.55)]
    if gs_len and 800 <= gs_len <= 5000:
       # insert? maybe after 2048
    for n,frac in stages:
       remaining=deadline-time.monotonic()
       alloc=frac*budget
       # if stage length n close to best length and best good? still run
       if remaining < 30: break
       t=min(alloc, remaining-30)
       if t < 10: continue
       init=_choose_initial(n,best)
       seq,score=_coordinate_search(init,t,seed=seed+n,verbose=True)
       if np.isfinite(score) and score < best_score:
           best=seq; best_score=score
           if verbose print
    # if time remains > 250 and best score >? run 8192
    remaining=deadline-time.monotonic()
    if remaining > 250 and best is not None:
       t=remaining-20
       # maybe if t > 1200 cap? no, use all.
       init=_choose_initial(8192,best)
       seq,score=_coordinate_search(init,t,seed=seed+9999,verbose=True)
       if score<best_score: best=seq; best_score=score
    # final fallback
    if best is None:
       best=_make_power(1024,0.5)
    best=_normalize_sequence(best)
    # Optional: call evaluate_sequence? It is available; but if our internal score good. We can call to verify, but costs one eval. Could do if time>2s and n<=8192.
    try:
       ev=float(evaluate_sequence(best.tolist()))
       if np.isfinite(ev): # no update needed
           best_official=ev
    except Exception:
       best_official=best_score
    return [float(x) for x in best]
```

def _minimize_coordinate(...):
    if S0 <= 1e-14:
        return old
    best_x=old; best_val = _coord_value(...old)
    f0=...
    if f0 < best_val: best...
    hi = max(S0, 4*old, 10/n, 1e-8)
    # If old is 0 and S0 ~1, hi=1
    for _ in range(12):
        fhi = _coord_value(...hi)
        if fhi < best_val:
            best_x=hi; best_val=fhi
            hi *= 2.0
        else:
            break
    # Now golden on [0,hi]
    a=0.0; b=hi
    c = b - _GOLDEN*(b-a)
    d = a + _GOLDEN*(b-a)
    fc=... c; fd=... d
    for _ in range(35):
       if b-a <= 1e-8*max(1.0,b): break
       if fc <= fd:
          b=d; d=c; fd=fc; c=b - _GOLDEN*(b-a); fc=_coord_value(c)
       else:
          a=c; c=d; fc=fd; d=a + _GOLDEN*(b-c); fd=_coord_value(d)
    x=0.5*(a+b); fx=...
    if fx<best_val: best...
    # Also try hi? fhi maybe <=best? Could if loop extended and best at hi; but after loop fhi>best probably. Try:
    if fhi < best_val: best=hi
    return best_x
```

def _make_random_smooth(n, seed):
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    y = rng.normal(0.0,1.0,n)
    # increase to have asymmetry? maybe add trend
    trend = np.linspace(0,1,n)
    z = y + 0.5*trend
    # smooth
    k = max(5,n//64)
    kernel = np.ones(k)/k
    zs = np.convolve(z, kernel, mode='same')
    a = np.exp(zs)
    return _normalize_sequence(a)
```

    cands=[]

Need avoid roundoff; FFT of nonnegative real may have tiny imaginary/negatives; clamp. For n=8192, FFT faster. Let's implement:
```
def _convolution_full(a):
    n=a.size
    N=2*n-1
    L=1
    while L < N: L <<=1

```

Let's implement `_convolution_full(a)`:
```
def _convolution_full(a):
    n = a.size
    N = 2*n - 1
    L=1
    while L < N:
       L <<=1
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    ca = np.fft.irfft(fa*fa, L)
    ca = ca[:N]
    # replace small negatives
    # ca[ca < 0] = 0.0 might mask actual? all should nonnegative.
    ca = np.maximum(ca, 0.0)
    return ca
```
Good.

```
def _smooth_perturb(a, rng, amp=0.15):
    n=a.size
    if n<3: return a
    k=max(3, n//64)
    ker=np.ones(k)/k
    noise=rng.normal(0.0, amp, n)
    noise=np.convolve(noise, ker, mode='same')
    a2=a*np.exp(noise)
    return _normalize_sequence(a2)
```

def _clean_array(a):
   try: arr=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64).ravel()
   except: return None
   arr=arr[np.isfinite(arr)]

```

def _normalize_sequence(a):
    arr = _clean_array(a)
    if arr is None:
        arr = np.ones(256)
    s=arr.sum()
    arr/=s
    return arr
```

def _coord_value(...):
    denom = S0 + x
    if denom <= 0.0: return float('inf')
    if x <= 1e-15:
        m = float(np.max(c0))
    else:
        np.multiply(h, x, out=tmp)
        tmp += c0
        m = float(tmp.max())
    sv = self_base + x*x
    if sv > m: m=sv
    return m/(denom*denom)
```

Let's implement `_fast_score(a)`:
```
def _fast_score(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size==0: return inf
    s=float(np.sum(a))
    if s <= 0 or not np.isfinite(s): return inf
    if a.size <= 512:
       c=np.convolve(a,a)
    else:
       c=_convolution_full(a)
    m=float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(m) or m < 0: return inf
    return float(2.0*a.size*m/(s*s))
```
For normalized candidate, s=1. For upsample normalized. Good.

Now, `_convolution_full` clamps negative; for a all zeros? not.

It could converge to internal high if function is increasing from 0 but minimum at boundary. This is a known issue: golden-section requires minimum interior and f unimodal; if minimum at boundary, it may still converge to boundary? Let's see: a=0,b=hi,c=b-rL,d=a+rL. If f increasing, fc > fd? Since c closer to b, f(c)>f(d), so else branch: a=c. This moves lower bound up, excluding 0! It will converge to d? Actually if increasing, minimum at a=0, but algorithm discards [a,c] including a, wrong. Standard golden for minimization assumes function decreases then increases; if monotone increasing, it does discard optimum at boundary. Need handle boundaries.

We must account boundary minima. We already know f0 and fhi values. During golden, we should keep best_x/best_val from boundary and candidate points, and at end return min of boundary/golden. But golden search result may be bad; min with f0/fhi okay. If boundary 0 is best, we keep it. If minimum at hi, keep fhi. If interior, golden should find. For monotone increasing, best remains 0. For monotone decreasing, best at hi? But function eventually increases, hi chosen beyond min; if hi after min, interior. If hi before min, extension should increase hi until fhi > best? Actually if function decreasing up to hi, fhi < f0, best=hi, extension doubles until fhi > current best? It updates best to hi and doubles; eventually fhi higher, best previous hi. Good. So boundaries fallback solves.

But golden on monotone increasing may waste but final min with f0 okay.

Thus in `_minimize_coordinate`, keep `best_x=0` if f0 best; and after golden compare. Good.

Also, if true minimum near 0 but not exactly, golden might discard? If function decreases slightly then increases near 0, interior; golden should keep if c/d values? If minimum very close to 0, c/d maybe both on increasing side, f(c)>f(d)? If d > c? Actually c near hi, d near 0. If minimum at d almost 0, f(d) low, f(c) high, fc>fd -> else a=c, keeps lower part [c,b]? Wait a becomes c (near hi), d? Hmm standard golden: if f(c) < f(d), minimum left of d -> b=d; else minimum right of c -> a=c. If f(c)>f(d), it sets a=c, discarding [a,c] where a=0. If minimum is between a=0 and c but >d? Wait points arranged a<c<d<b. If f(c)>f(d), possible minimum is left of c (including 0..c) or right of c? For unimodal decreasing then increasing, if f(c)>f(d), the minimum is to the right of c (since function decreasing at c,d), so discard left [a,c]. If minimum near 0, function increasing at 0, f(c)<f(d)? If increasing, f(c)>f(d) (c farther) -> algorithm discards left incorrectly. But boundary fallback keeps 0. What if minimum at small interior just right of 0 with function decreasing from 0 to min then increasing? Near 0 derivative negative? Then f(d) could be lower than f(c) and algorithm discards left but min is between 0 and c? For unimodal, if f(d)<f(c), min is right of c? Actually shape: decreasing until min then increasing. If min between 0 and c, then f(d) (right of c) > f(min). f(c) could be > f(d) if d is on increasing side but c on decreasing? Hard. Standard golden assumption holds for entire interval. Boundary fallback may not find interior very near 0 if discarded. To be safer, we can perform a ternary/golden that retains best evaluated, but if its search discards boundary, interior near boundary might be lost. - Also points: `old * [0.25,0.5,2,4]` within hi.

for x in cands: evaluate and update best
```

```python
"""
Coordinate-descent search for nonnegative sequences minimizing

    2*n*max(conv(a,a)) / sum(a)^2.

The search keeps the convolution incrementally updatable: when one entry a[i]
changes, only O(n) convolution entries change.  For each coordinate the
one-dimensional minimization problem is solved with a bounded golden-section
search plus boundary/grid candidates.  Several lengths are tried, starting from
skewed power-law profiles, with occasional smooth random perturbations to escape
local minima.
"""

import time
import numpy as np

_GOLDEN = 0.6180339887498949


def _clean_array(a):
    try:
        arr = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    except Exception:
        return None
    if arr.size == 0:
        return None
    arr = arr[np.isfinite(arr)]
    arr = arr[arr >= 0.0]
    if arr.size == 0:
        return None
    return arr


def _normalize_sequence(a):
    arr = _clean_array(a)
    if arr is None:
        arr = np.ones(256, dtype=np.float64)
    s = float(np.sum(arr))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-15:
        return np.ones(256, dtype=np.float64) / 256.0
    arr /= s
    return arr


def _convolution_full(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)
    if n <= 512:
        c = np.convolve(a, a)
        c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
        return c
    N = 2 * n - 1
    L = 1
    while L < N:
        L <<= 1
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    c = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, L)[:N]
    c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
    return c


def _fast_score(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return float("inf")
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-15:
        return float("inf")
    c = _convolution_full(a)
    m = float(np.max(c)) if c.size else 0.0
    if not np.isfinite(m) or m < 0.0:
        return float("inf")
    return float(2.0 * n * m / (s * s))


def _make_power(n, p):
    x = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) + 1.0
    if p == 0.0:
        a = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    else:
        a = x ** (-p)
    return _normalize_sequence(a)


def _make_rational(n, cpar):
    x = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) + 1.0
    a = 1.0 / (np.sqrt(x) + float(cpar))
    return _normalize_sequence(a)


def _upsample(a, target):
    a = _clean_array(a)
    if a is None:
        return _make_power(int(target), 0.5)
    target = int(target)
    m = a.size
    if m == target:
        return a.copy()
    if m == 1:
        return np.full(target, float(a[0]), dtype=np.float64)
    old_x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, m)
    new_x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, target)
    b = np.interp(new_x, old_x, a)
    return _normalize_sequence(b)


def _choose_initial(n, global_best):
    n = int(n)
    cands = []
    if global_best is not None:
        cands.append(_upsample(global_best, n))
    cands.append(_make_power(n, 0.5))
    if n <= 5000:
        cands.append(_make_power(n, 0.35))
        cands.append(_make_power(n, 0.25))
        cands.append(_make_rational(n, 3.0))
    else:
        cands.append(_make_power(n, 0.35))

    best = None
    best_score = float("inf")
    for a in cands:
        try:
            sc = _fast_score(a)
        except Exception:
            sc = float("inf")
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
            best = a
            best_score = sc
    if best is None:
        best = _make_power(n, 0.5)
    return best


def _coord_value(c0, h, self_base, S0, x, tmp):
    denom = S0 + x
    if denom <= 0.0:
        return float("inf")
    if x <= 1e-15:
        m = float(np.max(c0))
    else:
        np.multiply(h, x, out=tmp)
        tmp += c0
        m = float(tmp.max())
    sv = self_base + x * x
    if sv > m:
        m = sv
    return m / (denom * denom)


def _minimize_coordinate(c0, h, self_idx, self_base, S0, old, n, tmp):
    if S0 <= 1e-14:
        return old

    best_x = float(old)
    best_val = _coord_value(c0, h, self_base, S0, best_x, tmp)

    f0 = _coord_value(c0, h, self_base, S0, 0.0, tmp)
    if f0 < best_val:
        best_val = f0
        best_x = 0.0

    hi = max(float(S0), 4.0 * float(old), 10.0 / max(1, n), 1e-8)
    for _ in range(12):
        if hi > 1e6:
            break
        fhi = _coord_value(c0, h, self_base, S0, hi, tmp)
        if fhi < best_val:
            best_val = fhi
            best_x = hi
            hi *= 2.0
        else:
            break

    fhi = _coord_value(c0, h, self_base, S0, hi, tmp)
    if fhi < best_val:
        best_val = fhi
        best_x = hi

    # Grid candidates are very useful when the minimum is near a boundary.
    for frac in (0.0, 1e-4, 1e-3, 1e-2, 1e-1, 3e-1, 7e-1, 1.0):
        x = hi * frac
        if x < best_x and x <= hi:
            fx = _coord_value(c0, h, self_base, S0, x, tmp)
            if fx < best_val:
                best_val = fx
                best_x = x

    # Golden-section search on [0, hi].  Boundary/grid candidates above protect
    # against boundary minima even if the interior search moves away from them.
    a = 0.0
    b = hi
    c = b - _GOLDEN * (b - a)
    d = a + _GOLDEN * (b - a)
    fc = _coord_value(c0, h, self_base, S0, c, tmp)
    fd = _coord_value(c0, h, self_base, S0, d, tmp)

    for _ in range(30):
        if b - a <= 1e-7 * max(1.0, b):
            break
        if fc <= fd:
            b = d
            d = c
            fd = fc
            c = b - _GOLDEN * (b - a)
            fc = _coord_value(c0, h, self_base, S0, c, tmp)
        else:
            a = c
            c = d
            fc = fd
            d = a + _GOLDEN * (b - c)
            fd = _coord_value(c0, h, self_base, S0, d, tmp)

    x = 0.5 * (a + b)
    fx = _coord_value(c0, h, self_base, S0, x, tmp)
    if fx < best_val:
        best_val = fx
        best_x = x

    if best_x is None or not np.isfinite(best_x) or best_x < 0.0:
        return old
    return float(best_x)


def _smooth_perturb(a, rng, amp=0.2):
    n = a.size
    if n < 4:
        return a.copy()
    k = max(3, n // 64)
    kernel = np.ones(k, dtype=np.float64) / float(k)
    noise = rng.normal(0.0, float(amp), n)
    noise = np.convolve(noise, kernel, mode="same")
    a2 = a * np.exp(noise)
    return _normalize_sequence(a2)


def _coordinate_search(a, max_time, seed=1, verbose=False):
    start = time.monotonic()
    deadline = start + max(1.0, float(max_time))

    a = _normalize_sequence(a)
    n = int(a.size)
    if n < 2:
        sc = _fast_score(a)
        return a, sc

    c = _convolution_full(a).astype(np.float64, copy=False)
    S = 1.0
    best = a.copy()
    best_score = float(2.0 * n * np.max(c))

    if verbose:
        print(f"coord-search start: n={n} score={best_score:.9f}")

    N = 2 * n - 1
    h = np.empty(N, dtype=np.float64)
    c0 = np.empty(N, dtype=np.float64)
    tmpN = np.empty(N, dtype=np.float64)
    tmpn = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

    no_improve_passes = 0
    perturbures = 0
    max_perturbures = 4
    passes = 0

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        passes += 1

        S = float(np.sum(a))
        if not np.isfinite(S) or S <= 1e-14:
            a = _make_power(n, 0.5)
            c = _convolution_full(a).astype(np.float64, copy=False)
            S = 1.0
        else:
            scale = 1.0 / S
            a *= scale
            c *= scale * scale
            S = 1.0

        np.maximum(c, 0.0, out=c)
        cur_score = float(2.0 * n * np.max(c))
        if cur_score < best_score:
            best = a.copy()
            best_score = cur_score

        if time.monotonic() >= deadline:
            break

        order = rng.permutation(n)
        broke_on_time = False

        for idx, i in enumerate(order):
            if idx and (idx & 63) == 0:
                if time.monotonic() >= deadline:
                    broke_on_time = True
                    break

            i = int(i)
            old = float(a[i])
            S0 = S - old
            if S0 <= 1e-14:
                continue

            self_idx = 2 * i

            h.fill(0.0)
            np.add(a, a, out=tmpn)
            h[i:i + n] += tmpn
            h[self_idx] = 0.0

            if old != 0.0:
                np.multiply(h, old, out=tmpN)
                np.subtract(c, tmpN, out=c0)
                c0[self_idx] -= old * old
            else:
                c0[:] = c

            np.maximum(c0, 0.0, out=c0)
            self_base = float(c0[self_idx])

            x_new = _minimize_coordinate(
                c0, h, self_idx, self_base, S0, old, n, tmpN
            )

            if not np.isfinite(x_new) or x_new < 0.0:
                x_new = old

            delta = float(x_new - old)
            if abs(delta) > 1e-14:
                S += delta
                if delta != 0.0:
                    np.multiply(h, delta, out=tmpN)
                    c += tmpN
                    c[self_idx] += x_new * x_new - old * old
                a[i] = x_new

        S = float(np.sum(a))
        if not np.isfinite(S) or S <= 1e-14:
            a = _make_power(n, 0.5)
            c = _convolution_full(a).astype(np.float64, copy=False)
            S = 1.0
        else:
            scale = 1.0 / S
            a *= scale
            c *= scale * scale
            S = 1.0

        if passes % 10 == 0:
            c = _convolution_full(a).astype(np.float64, copy=False)
        np.maximum(c, 0.0, out=c)

        score = float(2.0 * n * np.max(c))
        if not np.isfinite(score):
            break

        if score < best_score * (1.0 - 1e-12):
            best = a.copy()
            best_score = score
            no_improve_passes = 0
        else:
            no_improve_passes += 1

        if verbose:
            print(
                f"coord-pass {passes} n={n} score={score:.9f} "
                f"best={best_score:.9f} elapsed={time.monotonic() - start:.1f}s"
            )

        if no_improve_passes == 3 and perturbures < max_perturbures:
            perturbures += 1
            if verbose:
                print(f"  perturbation {perturbures} n={n}")
            a = _smooth_perturb(a, rng, amp=0.25)
            c = _convolution_full(a).astype(np.float64, copy=False)
            no_improve_passes = 0

        if no_improve_passes >= 6:
            break
        if broke_on_time:
            break

    if best is None or not np.all(np.isfinite(best)):
        best = _make_power(n, 0.5)
        best_score = _fast_score(best)

    if verbose:
        print(f"coord-search done: n={n} best={best_score:.9f} passes={passes}")

    return best, best_score


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1800, **kwargs):
    start = time.monotonic()
    budget = max(1.0, float(budget_s) - 20.0)
    deadline = start + budget

    best = None
    best_score = float("inf")

    # Use a previously supplied construction if it is valid and useful.
    gs = globals().get("height_sequence_1", None)
    if gs is not None:
        try:
            garr = _normalize_sequence(gs)
            if 128 <= garr.size <= 10000:
                sc = _fast_score(garr)
                if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
                    best = garr
                    best_score = sc
        except Exception:
            pass

    # Main staged search.  The last large stage is often the best.
    stage_plan = [
        (1024, 0.05),
        (2048, 0.15),
        (4096, 0.52),
    ]

    try:
        for n, frac in stage_plan:
            remaining = deadline - time.monotonic()
            alloc = frac * budget
            t = min(alloc, remaining - 30.0)
            if t < 15.0:
                break

            init = _choose_initial(n, best)
            try:
                seq, sc = _coordinate_search(
                    init,
                    t,
                    seed=int(seed) + 1000 * int(n),
                    verbose=True,
                )
                if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
                    best = seq
                    best_score = sc
            except Exception:
                pass

            if best is not None and best_score < 1.25:
                break

        # Extra very long run if there is meaningful time left.
        remaining = deadline - time.monotonic()
        if remaining > 220.0 and best is not None:
            t = remaining - 25.0
            init = _choose_initial(8192, best)
            try:
                seq, sc = _coordinate_search(
                    init,
                    t,
                    seed=int(seed) + 777777,
                    verbose=True,
                )
                if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
                    best = seq
                    best_score = sc
            except Exception:
                pass

        # If still above the target, try a different skew on a moderate length.
        remaining = deadline - time.monotonic()
        if remaining > 180.0 and best_score > 1.5027:
            t = remaining - 20.0
            init = _make_power(4096, 0.35)
            try:
                seq, sc = _coordinate_search(
                    init,
                    t,
                    seed=int(seed) + 314159,
                    verbose=True,
                )
                if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
                    best = seq
                    best_score = sc
            except Exception:
                pass
    except Exception:
        pass

    if best is None or not np.all(np.isfinite(best)):
        best = _make_power(1024, 0.5)

    best = _normalize_sequence(best)
    best = np.clip(best, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(np.sum(best))
    if s <= 0 or not np.isfinite(s):
        best = _make_power(1024, 0.5)
    else:
        best = np.clip(best / s, 0.0, 1000.0)

    return [float(x) for x in best]
```
